N90 · Two 1804 Lincoln deeds still leave Welch’s Tennessee identity unresolved

Reviewed 30 September 2026 · Two original deed readings and an exact film-series/pointer correction

**Thomas Welch of Lincoln County, North Carolina**, sold Conrad Heldebrand of the same place one hundred acres on Buffalo Creek for £100 lawful North Carolina money, **13 October 1804**. The deed names boundary lands of John Taylor, Seebaugh and the widow Murphy. Witnesses are Sam. Ramsey and William Fouts; January 1805 proof by Samuel Ramsey orders registration without an exact recording day. No wife, Rachel, Norman, Aaron, Tennessee residence or departure statement occurs in the target deed.

Exact original: Lincoln deed volume 22, pp. 287–289, [DGS 7527157 image 155](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3QSQ-G98W-3QX9) and [image 156](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3QS7-L98W-3Q8Y). The [filming title at image 7](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3QS7-L98W-37H8) labels volume 22, 1803–1805, pp. 1–391. Six new images were opened for wrapper/title, calibration and the complete target; five downloads were retained, not five fully read deeds. Zero name/discovery queries were run. The T-shaped “his mark” is a recorded mark, not a middle initial or unique autograph. The absence of a dower clause does not establish unmarried status or Rachel’s death. The five-year interval permits later migration but does not prove it.

The first [catalogue 77350](https://www.familysearch.org/en/search/catalog/koha:77350) film-row audit found no volume 23 row, without proving loss or scan absence. The successful successor through [catalogue 399872](https://www.familysearch.org/en/search/catalog/399872), film 19256 / DGS 7513706, resolves that access gap: [title image 211](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3QS7-L983-NH66) labels Record Deeds No. 23, 1805–08. The exact p. 251 / image 344 test did not contain the reported Bellinger deed. That derivative pointer remains mismatched; the whole book is no longer unlocated.

**The p. 256 successor succeeds:** [DB23 pp. 256–257, image 347](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3QS7-9983-NH38), records Thomas Welch of Lincoln conveying **150 acres on Indian Creek for $350, 22 October 1804**. The buyer’s given name Jacob is clear; the surname remains qualified as **Dell[...]**, apparently the newsletter’s Dellinger. Boundaries name Welch’s own land, **Peregreen Norman**, a separate McNamar, Carruth and Sleagle. Do not expand Peregreen into Forney/Firney Green or merge the Norman and McNamar names. Witnesses read John McNemar and Sam. Jenkins; proof is October Sessions 1807, not an exact registration day. The copied X mark differs from the Buffalo deed’s copied T shape; neither is an autograph, and the difference proves neither distinct sellers nor identity. No wife, Rachel, Aaron or Tennessee residence appears. One new original leaf, zero discovery queries and its complete target deed were read in this successor; the adjoining separate Welch opening was outside that scope. [The completed successor](./index.html#N93) now identifies 200 acres for $50, correcting the earlier incidental acreage description without changing this 150-acre result.

The original supplies a specific Lincoln Welch–Peregreen Norman boundary association, without identifying the North Carolina and Tennessee Normans or Welchs as the same people. Different given names remain distinct; the newsletter’s warning of multiple Thomas Welchs still matters. Neither October deed excludes migration before 1809 or supplies an Aaron bridge.

N91 · Brown acquisition originals do not yet reconstruct Aaron’s title

Reviewed 30 September 2026 · Two finite original-index scopes and two exact successors; five candidate deeds read

The existing Greene JJ12–13 purchase, [DGS 8356253 image 13](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CSYD-ZQWV-S), records Edmund and William Brown to Aaron, all of Greene, **25 December 1823, 245¾ acres on Shoulderbone, $737.25**. It gives no predecessor grantor or original grant. This parcel remains distinct from the approximately 230-acre Hancock Woodard obligation. Five newly examined candidate deeds provide comparison, without a backward Aaron link:

- [GG319, DGS 7898897 image 171](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CS42-94M9-J): **Benjamin G. Brown of Jones County to Edmund Brown of Greene, 10 February 1819, $150, one-third interest in a fifty-acre Shoulderbone tract**, recorded 29 February 1820. Original expanded name corrects provisional index initials. Its white oak/maple/pine/ash and pole-branch polygon differs from Aaron’s. Jones is the grantor’s residence, not Aaron’s; no Brown kinship is stated.

- [Book 3 pp. 626–627, DGS 7898894 image 333](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CS42-SSKS-2): Francis Blackwood to **Edward Brown**, both Greene, 7 February 1803, twenty acres, $80. The original corrects the ambiguous index given name; this is not verified Edmund. A 2 April boundary supplement names Edward’s and Daniel Kelly’s adjoining land and an unnamed creek, not verified Shoulderbone. Proof/registration are 21 June 1803.

- [Book 3 pp. 448–449, DGS 7898894 image 235](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CS42-SS6W-S): John Long to **William Brown**, both Greene, 26 December 1800, 290 acres on Richland Creek, $600; wife Margaret Long relinquishes dower. Wm. Hill, J.P., and Francis Blackwood witness; registered 11 March 1803. Hugh Jones and Frederick Ashfield are boundary associates. A grant in Long’s name is recited for 13 December, its year tentatively read as 1795. This is not the same entire tract by watercourse, acreage and polygon; a later subdivision or indirect chain is not excluded. Identity with the 1823 William remains unproved.

- [Book 1 p. 341, DGS 7898893 image 179](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CS42-VBL4), and [p. 342, image 180](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CS42-VBPY): **Benjamin Porter of Richmond County and Paty Claiborne his wife to William Brown of Greene, 29 December 1786, 423 acres, £100 sterling**. The copy later writes Paty C. Porter; these literal forms do not independently establish a maiden-name pedigree. Witnesses are Thos. Porter and Thomas Read; Richmond acknowledgment/dower is 9 July 1787, registration 15 July 1789. The original gives a Porter grant and a different polygon, without a named watercourse, neighbor or grant date. Richmond is Porter’s residence, not Aaron’s. A whole-tract match is not demonstrated; subdivision and William’s identity remain unresolved. Register names are not authenticated autographs.

- [DGS 7898893 image 475](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CS42-V1D4) and [continuation image 476](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CS42-V16Y): **Andrew and Elizabeth Armour, his wife, of Greene to William Brown of the same place, 15 February 1795, 504 acres on Richland Creek, £103 10 shillings sterling**. The line surveyed by E. Parks for James Whatley and Howel/Ogletree/Swansy boundaries are associate pointers, not kinship. Witnesses read David Lockhan and James Armour; proof is 15 July, registration 16 September 1795. The leading manuscript page digit remains uncertain, so exact ARK/image citations control. No Aaron or prior residence appears; the whole tract differs from Aaron’s Shoulderbone acreage/polygon, while subdivision and William’s identity remain open. Copied names are not autograph specimens.

DGS 7898892 reverse/grantee images 20–27 were screened for selected Brown names; direct images 25–27 were additionally screened. The earlier reverse interval 12–19 comprises B leaves 16–19, blank boundary leaves 14–15 and A-section leaves 12–13. Dark lower references limit some readings. There were no full-text discovery queries; a brief image-19 machine transcript aid did not resolve the original bottom row. This is not a complete Brown index or title-chain negative. Twenty-five full JPGs were retained across both scopes: sixteen index spreads, three target deeds and six navigation spreads. Retention does not mean every instrument was read.

The Porter successor corrects the provisional p. 324 pointer to **p. 341** and replaces the index wife-name shorthand with the literal original forms. Its four retained spreads include two navigation leaves. The Armour successor resolves the year as **1795** over the initial 1794 reading, without promoting the shadowed index page guesses. Six navigation spreads and two target spreads were saved. The initial Brown Arm* query returned 154 OR-style results; one required-term **+Brown +Arm*** query in DGS 7898893 narrowed this to eleven. The first six snippets were screened before selecting the exact fifth entry; the complete target and continuation were read, not all eleven results. [The separately completed William Brown-to-Francis Blackwood disposal](./index.html#N94), image 612, now supplies an original reading rather than only a machine transcript. It is a disposal comparison, not a sixth acquisition candidate or an established Aaron title link. Its nearby Armour-to-Jane Haw instrument must not be joined to the Brown conveyance. No Aaron title chain, prior home or kinship follows from the five candidate deeds.

N92 · The known Walthall debt case now has an exact loose-file locator

Reviewed 30 September 2026 · Catalogue advance and correction; bound writs were already read on 28 September

The Georgia Archives catalogue lists [“Maltall, Richard v. Aaron Oliphent [Oliphant], Oct. 1826”](https://georgiaarchives.as.atlas-sys.com/repositories/2/archival_objects/406905): **Hancock Superior Court 170-01-001, RCB 65388, C 613886, object 406905**. This is a new exact retrieval locator for the probable loose counterpart of already known litigation. The folder remains unread. Its title date must not be confused with the [whole series’ 1794–1956 span and 119-cubic-foot extent](https://georgiaarchives.as.atlas-sys.com/repositories/2/resources/2616). Unrestricted catalogue access does not confirm remote copying or a fee.

The already-read bound record was downloaded and reread: [DGS 8629258 image 43, pp. 72–73](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-C3H3-5BSP), and [image 44, pp. 74–75](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-C3H3-5BC8). Richard Waltall/Walthall, bearer, sought Aaron’s $200 sealed note to Jonathan Woodward or bearer, dated 26 September 1825, due 25 December next. Judgment is 12 October 1826; execution is 14 December; Richard Baugh pays costs. A 26 January 1827 levy names a note on **Samuel Ship**, not the different estate debtor Lemuel. Apparent service on 15 March versus process on 16 March 1826 remains unresolved. Later process or execution does not prove Aaron survived to those dates. The copied note is not an authenticated autograph.

The note and Woodard land bond may be reciprocal obligations, but neither explicitly says the $200 pays for the 230 acres. That was already an inference in the 28 September reading. The catalogue spelling, defendant, place and term support probable correlation, not an original-folder identification. No newly discovered court fact, earlier residence or parentage is counted from this repeat.

One official Oliphant catalogue query returned seventeen titles/descriptions, all screened. Aaron’s detail and parent series were opened. Four later Solomon titles were retained only as catalogue references: 1853 bearer case C613901; Naomi Freeny/Freeney proceedings C634569 and C640200; and an 1857 State case C730453. No charge, outcome, occupation, violence, flight or identity bridge is established; these do not corroborate the family’s vague rumor. The separate film-limited Oliph* search returned three snippets, of which only the two known bound leaves were read. No complete county court search is claimed.

The next record is the complete loose C613886 folder, including reverses, wrapper, note and endorsements if present. An exact copy-enquiry draft is unsent; no archive message, order or payment occurred. Compare its contents with the bound record before claiming additional evidence.

The separate deed beginning at the foot of Lincoln volume 23 p. 257 is now read through p. 258 and its court proof. **Thomas Welch of Lincoln County, North Carolina, to Jacob Dell[...] of the same place, 22 October 1804: 200 acres on Indian Creek for $50.** Fifty dollars is consideration, correcting the earlier incidental “50 acres” pointer description. The preceding 150-acre deed in [N90](./index.html#N90) is unaffected. Buyer and boundary surname readings remain qualified; no wife or kinship is stated. Copied witnesses are John McNemar and Sam. Jenkins, with October Sessions 1807 proof by McNemar. The X mark is a register copy, not an authenticated autograph.

Exact source: [catalogue 399872](https://www.familysearch.org/en/search/catalog/399872), film 19256 / DGS 7513706, DB23 pp. 257–258, opening on [image 347](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3QS7-9983-NH38) and continuation/proof on [image 348, left](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3QS7-L983-NHMT). The distinct 12 October / p. 251 Bellinger pointer remains mismatched.

**The adjacent p. 259 deed names Nicholas, not Thomas:** Martin Shidle to Anthony Shidle, both Lincoln, **16 June 1807, 200 acres on Buffalo Fork of Indian Creek for $10**, explicitly recites a patent to **Nicholas Welch, 22 May 1772**. John Deel witnesses and proves it in October 1807; no exact registration day is supplied. The complete target was newly enlarged on the right side of the same image 348, with no additional original download. An earlier small-image impression of Thomas / 1782 is withdrawn. This is a later deed recital; the 1772 patent itself remains unread, and no Nicholas–Thomas relationship is supplied. Equal acreage and related creek names do not establish the same parcel; the recorded polygons differ and an intervening chain was not investigated.

Neither deed names Aaron, a Tennessee residence or an interstate migration. No parentage, unique Welch identity or tree relationship follows. The complete [1809 loose Welch will](./index.html#R09) remains the direct Tennessee identity test, with its prepared request unsent.

N94 · Brown’s 1797 Richland disposal supplies comparison, not Aaron’s title chain

Reviewed 30 September 2026 · One complete recorded deed; day reading retained as uncertain

In **January 1797**, William Brown of Greene County, Georgia, conveyed **120 acres on Richland Creek to Francis Blackwood of the same place for £50**. The index day reads 13; the first enlarged original reading was 18. The exact day remains unresolved after the direct-index review in [N95](./index.html#N95); neither is silently substituted into the frozen earlier memo. The deed names a survey line run by Ezekiel Park and copied witnesses James Thompson and Isaac McClendon, J.P. No wife, Aaron, previous residence, named relative or predecessor grantor is supplied. Omission of a wife does not establish unmarried status.

Exact original: [Greene catalogue 197862](https://www.familysearch.org/en/search/catalog/koha:197862), film 158294 / DGS 7898893, [image 612, replacement pp. 418–419](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CS42-V143). The entire Brown instrument, from lower left to upper right, was enlarged and read. One full spread was saved; reading crops are derivatives, not additional originals. The adjacent Simonton proof and Armour-to-Jane Haw deed are separate instruments. No Brown proof or registration statement appears before the next deed begins, and neighboring dates must not be imported. This is a register copy, not an autograph deed.

Blackwood also witnesses the [1800 Long-to-William Brown acquisition](./index.html#N91), but the 1797 disposal precedes that purchase and cannot be its direct sale without another instrument. The 1795 Armour acquisition and this disposal share Richland and a Park/Parks survey-line reference, without an explicit subdivision or source-deed link. The 120-acre Richland description differs from Aaron’s 245¾-acre Shoulderbone purchase as a whole tract; later subdivision or recombination is not excluded. William’s identity across the records remains unproved. The five acquisition candidates in N91 remain a separate count.

This scope used no new discovery query and substantively read only the Brown target, not a whole grantor index. [The completed bounded grantor-index successor](./index.html#N95) reads two further disposal deeds without establishing an explicit Shoulderbone parcel or predecessor link. N95 — Two more Brown disposals after a bounded grantor-index screen

Reviewed 30 September 2026 · Two complete recorded deeds; bounded index coverage

Nine previously unread left/direct B-index leaves, DGS 7898892 images 16–24, were screened from retained originals. This is a bounded name-column pass, with dark and overwritten portions, not a complete grantor-index or county negative. Reverse leaves, previously screened direct images 25–27 and later-recorded older interests remain outside this scope.

The first selected deed conveys **300 Richland Creek acres for $1,000 on 1 March 1804, William Brown to Reuben Brown**, both stated of Greene. It describes part of 575 acres originally granted to Henry Kerr in 1785; that grant remains unread. The original corrects the index’s apparent January execution month. Proof and registration are dated 2 April 1804. Exact source: Greene book 4 pp. 18–19, film 158295 / DGS 7898894, [image 409](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CS42-SSV4-T). The entire instrument was read. The copied X mark is not an autograph; a shared surname does not establish the Browns’ relationship.

The second conveys **200 acres at the headwaters of Town Creek for £80 on 2 November 1794, William Brown and Sally his wife to David Peoples junior**, both parties stated of Greene. The original corrects the preliminary Peebler buyer reading and page reference. Older book 2 pp. 313–314 correspond to replacement pp. 308–309, film 158294 / DGS 7898893: [image 557, opening](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CS42-V1HQ) and [image 558, continuation](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CS42-V1HW). Proof is 13 January 1795; registration is 14 June 1796. The body repeatedly names Sally as his wife, while the copied execution spelling is less clear. Her maiden name, this William’s identity with Aaron’s seller and the relationship implied by “junior” remain unknown. The copied marks are not autograph specimens.

Recovery used one required-term +Brown +Sally query restricted to DGS 7898893, returning seven results. Only the strongest matching target and its necessary opening were read; no variant grid or complete result-record screen followed. Eight new full JPGs were retained: three target spreads for two complete deeds and five navigation scans. Nine index reading crops reuse earlier originals and are not new original downloads.

The direct index gives 13 January 1797 for [the Blackwood disposal](./index.html#N94), against the earlier original day initially read as 18. The exact day remains unresolved after this index review; January 1797 is the current qualified date. Together N94 and this note record **three disposal deeds**, separately from [five acquisition candidates](./index.html#N91). Richland, Town Creek, acreage differences and copied marks do not establish a common William identity, Aaron’s Shoulderbone title chain, kinship or earlier home. The original Henry Kerr 1785 grant is a concrete next comparison for the Richland candidate, not an established Aaron origin lead.

N96 — Two exact Robert notice pointers remain original-reading targets

Reviewed 30 September 2026 · AI-generated obituary indexes and bounded official-index screen; zero originals read

A focused obituary-index search identified two **R. E. Oliphant, age 80, Marietta** candidates: *The Daily Ardmoreite*, **18 November 1958**, and *The Daily Oklahoman*, **19 November 1958**. Initials, approximate 1878 birth and residence fit the reported Robert Emmett lead, but the index explicitly warns that its facts were generated using artificial intelligence and may contain errors. Neither original has been read. These publication dates differ from the earlier 17 November 1959 cemetery/working-date lead; an exact death day, full identity and family relationships remain unresolved.

- [Daily Ardmoreite index, collection 61843 / record 1046711095](https://www.ancestry.ca/search/collections/61843/records/1046711095): [exact original image 902385666, identified article](https://www.newspapers.com/image/902385666/?article=2382136e-dff0-4a00-8d98-480b5d97d7d6&focus=0.15517032,0.5798576,0.27197027,0.66379994&xid=3355), 18 November 1958.

- [Daily Oklahoman index, collection 61843 / record 507420100](https://www.ancestry.ca/search/collections/61843/records/507420100): [exact original image 449658691, identified article](https://www.newspapers.com/image/449658691/?article=60ad9eea-4315-4225-8d24-7ddec4e6e1c6&focus=0.009919636,0.69202024,0.13719377,0.764218&xid=3355), 19 November 1958.

The first original reached security verification; the second exact URL was inaccessible through the public web tool. These are access limits, not newspaper absence. The two indexes may reproduce the same report, so they are not independent corroboration. Their inferred birth, death-place and relationship fields are not adopted as original wording; missing indexed child fields do not establish lifetime childlessness.

**Later access check, 30 September 2026:** human verification cleared in an authenticated session, but the exact Ardmoreite viewer stated “Upgrade to a Publisher Extra Subscription to view this page.” The original remains unread. [Ancestry Canada’s official All Access description](https://help.ancestry.ca/hc/en-ca/articles/53933280003987-All-Access-Membership) includes Newspapers.com Basic; that does not satisfy this page’s Publisher Extra requirement. This updates the access condition without replacing the earlier two-attempt history.

A separate official [OK2Explore death-index search](https://ok2explore.health.ok.gov/App/DeathSearch) used surname Oliphant, year 1959 and a ±1-year range, with other fields blank. All five returned entries were read; none matched Robert Emmett/E or the 17 November 1959 Love County lead. The results URL is stateful: reproduce those fields. This is a bounded 1958–1960 index negative, not death-certificate absence.

The finite batch completed four queries, two selected index-detail opens and two exact original-access attempts, with zero newspaper originals read or downloaded. Only the first two strong candidates from the ranked obituary result page were selected; no full 390-result screen is claimed. The next test is the complete 18 November Ardmoreite notice, with the 19 November Oklahoman as a distinct alternate, before interpreting relatives or matching the adult [Emmett household](./index.html#N73). The Andrew/Alfred head-name conflict and any later father–son continuation remain unresolved. No Aaron origin, tree relationship or historical DNA anchor changes.

N97 — Devaney spelling variants and DNA matches

Reviewed 30 September 2026 · Initial account/search audit, later service triangle and direct joined comparison; no new original family record

A read-only audit verifies two previously reported surname searches while distinguishing typed queries, exact ancestral-surname fields and independent families. The earlier Devaney and Devane screens yielded **fifteen displayed match identities after overlap**, rather than twenty-seven independent confirmations. The new Devanney result is already in both sets. The wider claim of matches under essentially every spelling has not been independently established.

Spelling and inspection status

| Spelling | Observed coverage | What that establishes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Devaney | Earlier thirteen-result screen; all results read. | Name-or-ancestral-surname hits, not thirteen independent paternal families. |
| Devane | Earlier fourteen-result screen; all read, twelve overlapping Devaney. | Fifteen displayed identities across the two searches; some exact Devane ancestry fields were inspected. |
| Devanney | One result; its detail explicitly lists both Devaney and Devanney. | An already observed tester, not a new tester or demonstrated new ancestral family. |
| Devine | Twenty-four results; only the first twenty read, with four unread. | A query-result count, not twenty-four verified exact Devine ancestors or independent lines. |
| Deviney | Exact ancestral-surname field verified in the strongest new Devine-query detail. | A submitted surname lead; no exact Devine entry appeared in that expanded list and no segment was assigned to Deviney. |
| Other reported spellings | A complete exact spelling/tester inventory remains unextracted. | No verified universal spelling set or independent-family count. |

The twenty inspected Devine rows include the previously identified mother-and-two-sons group, which must not count as three independent distant-family discoveries. Other shared-tree contexts also flag possible dependence without proving exact relationships. Historical joins use display names, DNA amounts and tree context rather than a complete stable-kit identity audit. Names in submitted ancestry can occur on different branches or coexist within one pedigree; a search hit does not identify the ancestor responsible for shared DNA.

The initial audit performed two queries and two match-detail reviews, with **no new triangulation in that scope**. Earlier service-confirmed triangles and negative tests retain their separate limits in [N41](./index.html#N41); reproducing saved export rows was not a new service test.

The pattern is compatible with an older overseas connection, a later American connection, another shared branch or a mixture. Spelling diversity alone does not distinguish those possibilities, date migration, identify an immigrant generation or establish a former paternal surname. No calibrated enrichment statistic is available without comparable tree coverage, independent-family grouping and ancestral/segment assignments. The [Y67 comparator](./index.html#C01) and [separate R-A823 comparator](./index.html#C02) remain distinct leads requiring their own identity and finer-result checks.

**Later targeted segment test, 30 September 2026:** the strongest new candidate’s ordinary pairwise export supplied one existing segment. One direct service comparison with the reported paternal cousin explicitly confirmed **a 24.4 cM triangulated segment**, with the full interval, selected participants and 2 cM filter checked in the expanded table. This is a new service result, not an inference from coordinate overlap. Conditional on the reported cousin identification, it strengthens a paternal-family autosomal lead through either paternal grandparent; no responsible Deviney ancestor, Aaron connection, exclusive male-line assignment or pre-American date is established.

**Completed joined comparison, 30 September 2026:** one direct four-person service test now confirms an **11.5 cM shared triangulated interval on chromosome 3** with both candidates and the reported paternal cousin. This resolves the overlap question: the earlier and new triangles concern one joined segment group, not independent confirmations of a Devaney ancestor. The responsible shared ancestor remains unidentified, and paternal-family attribution still depends on the reported cousin relationship. A documented common ancestral couple would be required for attribution, with both paternal-grandparent branches checked. [The Dunlop–Deveney comparison](./index.html#N99) and [Pilcher continuity evidence](./index.html#N100) remain subsidiary tests; further match-pedigree expansion is currently deferred while direct Aaron records and the exact bilateral Y comparison take priority. The spelling audit’s query counts, four unread Devine results and independence limits remain unchanged. [Spelling/family controls](./index.html#N46), [the competing grandmother branch](./index.html#N48) and [statistical limits](./index.html#N32) remain relevant.

N99 — A Dunlap–Deveney marriage and the pension family

Reviewed 30 September 2026 · Submitted pedigree, marriage index and bounded pension-file readings

These are subsidiary tests of DNA matches’ submitted ancestries, not documentation of Aaron Oliphant’s family or locations.

A submitted pedigree identifies **Sarah Black Deviney (reported 1791–1862)** as the wife of **Ephraim Henry Dunlap II (reported 1785–1862)**, claims a **21 February 1811 Rutherford County, North Carolina marriage**, and names her parents as **Aaron Deviney Jr. and Sarah Black**. These names, dates and connecting generations are tree assertions. [Restricted pedigree audit](https://drive.google.com/file/d/1sENs14t6gn7B5g0M1FwE4ihjP4RTzwhI/view).

Ancestry's marriage index now reports **Ephraim Dunlop and Sally B. Deveney**, **21 February 1811**, **Rutherford County, North Carolina**. The date, place and groom provide a precise comparison with the submitted marriage; **B. is not expanded to Black**. The citation is **County Court Records Rutherfordton, NC and FHL # 6418151**. An exact FamilySearch catalogue lookup returned no result for that number. No original bond/register, parent, bondsman or witness was exposed. Earlier bounded FamilySearch and collection 4802 queries found no target; they do not override this positive entry in collection 8909. [Indexed entry, record 2727795](https://www.ancestry.ca/search/collections/8909/records/2727795).

The previously read **S8321 pension certificate**, independently rechecked and preserved, explicitly names **Sally Dunlap** among Aaron Diviny's surviving children. It does not give Sally's husband, birth date or middle name. The certificate omits Anne, although a separate affidavit in the file identifies her as a daughter; that discrepancy remains unresolved. A **1936 derivative abstract** separately lists Sarah born **24 February 1786** and Sarah Black Deveny born **1 February 1791**. These are distinct entries, and neither is yet identified with Sally or the submitted Sarah. The pension material does not establish the wife's maiden name Black. [Original certificate, image 12](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3QHK-B7MP-B9S2-V); [later abstract, image 17](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3QHK-B7MP-B9SL-G).

Three adjacent leaves, images **13–15**, were newly read. Their **1937 summary** repeats the two Sarah entries and Sally Dunlap, includes Anne, and supplies no husband or Sarah/Sally bridge. It reports the wife's birth as **26 October 1748**, differing from the earlier 1936 abstract reading of **28 October**; this remains unresolved. The summaries derive from the same file and are not independent family attestations. [1937 summary](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3QHK-R7MP-TSD5-K); [family continuation](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3QHK-R7MP-TSD5-1); [1936 cover letter](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3QHK-17MP-YDQD).

The indexed bride is a meaningful candidate for pension daughter Sally Dunlap. The next original test must preserve the actual record/date type and any surety, witness or relationship wording. The official **CR.086.6 series, record 961137**, lists **CR.086.MA, Rutherford Marriage Records, 1774–1995**; This catalogue pass stopped before opening its child detail or containers. That range does not prove survival of this 1811 entry or crosswalk 6418151. FamilySearch collection 2524877's inspected twenty-row folder table contained no Rutherford row, a limited table result rather than record absence. [Official series and child listing](https://appx.archives.ncdcr.gov/solrDetailPages/series/NCA/Series_detail.html?fq=seriesRid:961137); [FamilySearch collection](https://www.familysearch.org/en/search/collection/2524877).

The pensioner was the Pennsylvania-born 1747 Aaron Deveny, a different man from Aaron Oliphant who died by 1826. The bride's identity, the later descent and the submitted parents remain unproved. [Pensioner's declaration, image 4](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3QHK-87MP-TT8Y).

The confirmed chromosome 3 group has no documented shared historical ancestral couple. Charlotte's claimed Samuel/Polly parentage remains unproved; Hazel's branch and other spouses remain alternatives. No person in this submitted route is established as Taylor's ancestor. This female-line route does not place the Oliphant Y line or establish a surname transition. The separately assigned Pilcher continuity test retains its own source limits.

**Later bond access and reading, 30 September 2026:** the official NC catalogue identifies **CR.086.MB / MF-C.086.60003, Marriage Bonds Vols. Ca–Cl, Co–Cu, D, E, 1779–1868**. FamilySearch [catalogue 1909432](https://www.familysearch.org/en/search/catalog/koha:1909432) exposes an accessible **Cabariss–Evans reel, film 2447753 / DGS 7740372**. Their coverage corresponds, but no inspected metadata proves the numerical crosswalk or resolves the index citation 6418151. A separate continuous screen of **images 582–600** passed through Doughty, Dowdle, Draper, Driskill, Drum and Duck to Earley/Early without a recognizable indexed Dunlop–Deveney couple. Nine locating frames and the nineteen-frame block overlap at three images: **twenty-five distinct images were observed and twenty-four viewer screenshots retained**, with no archival image download or target bond obtained. This is a bounded negative, not all D surnames, the entire 823-image film or the archive. N100 — Thomas Moses Pilcher’s death locator and a birth conflict

Reviewed 30 September 2026 · Death and parent indexes plus later original census/draft readings; death certificate and original SSA application unread

These Florida records test a DNA match’s submitted ancestry; they do not document Aaron Oliphant’s family or residence, and no evidence places Aaron in Florida.

The Florida death index supplies **Thomas Moses Pilcher, 1944, Bay County**, with no month/day, certificate number, parent or spouse in the inspected entry. A linked Greenwood Cemetery photograph visibly reads **Thomas M. Pilcher, 18 March 1882–17 December 1944**. The index and marker are plausible counterparts, but their identity correlation remains unproved; the official index does not independently establish the marker's exact dates. The marker's 1882 birth conflicts with the **March 1880** Thomas M. recorded as a son in the previously read 1900 Thomas J./Miriam household. That conflict cannot be resolved by overwriting one record or automatically merging or excluding the men. [Official-derived death index](https://www.ancestry.ca/search/collections/7338/records/213653); [memorial and photograph](https://www.findagrave.com/memorial/32793811/thomas-m-pilcher).

The separately inspected **Social Security Applications and Claims index** names **Morris R. / Morris Rudolph Pilcher's father as Thomas M. Pilcher and mother as Mary M. Crutchfield**. This strengthens the proposed later family correlation with the 1935 census, which calls Morris Tom's son and names his wife Malissa. The index reports Morris's birth as **22 November 1915**, conflicting with the submitted 1914 date. It supplies no birth or own parents for Thomas and does not expand his initial to Moses. The linked Thomas relative entry represents the same administrative source as Morris's applicant entry; it is not Thomas's own application or an independent second confirmation. No original application was exposed. [Morris's parent fields](https://www.ancestry.ca/search/collections/60901/records/32491658); [dependent Thomas relative entry](https://www.ancestry.ca/search/collections/60901/records/632491658).

The death-locator pass screened the first **50 of 441** fuzzy results, two details and one photograph; the successor read two exact Social Security details and the collection description, with no new query. The marker, memorial and cemetery index remain dependent. No death certificate was read or ordered. The exact **1944 Bay County death certificate** remains a parent/informant target; **17 December** is presently a cemetery-source date. No certificate number was exposed in that initial pass. This batch identified the exact **Thomas Moses Pilcher World War I draft entry**, collection **6482**, record **858741**, as a further test of birth, residence and nearest-relative fields. No common DNA ancestor, completed descent or new tree relationship follows. [Exact draft target](https://www.ancestry.ca/search/collections/6482/records/858741).

**Later original reading, 30 September 2026:** the exact [Thomas Moses Pilcher draft card, image 005150515_00886](https://www.ancestry.ca/imageviewer/collections/6482/images/005150515_00886?pId=858741), has now been read on both sides. Registered **12 September 1918**, it records **age 37**, birth **18 March 1881**, residence at **Graceville, Jackson County, Florida**, and nearest relative **Malissa Pilcher** at the same address. His signature appears. The card does not state Malissa's relationship or name his parents; Graceville is an address, not a birthplace.

The full name and nearest-relative name strengthen the proposed adult-family correlation. The recorded **1881** birth remains alongside the 1900 household's **March 1880** and the marker's **18 March 1882**. It is a registrant's report of his birth, not a birth record or a resolution of those conflicts. Coverage was one exact detail and one original scan containing front and reverse, with zero queries. The adult-to-1900-son bridge, actual death certificate and common DNA ancestor remain unresolved; no new tree relationship follows.

**Later household reading, 30 September 2026:** the exact [1910 original, image 31111_4327449-00589](https://www.ancestry.ca/imageviewer/collections/7884/images/31111_4327449-00589?pId=3009975), Jackson County, Florida, Graceville Town, **sheet 25B, lines 73–76**, records **T.M. Pilcher (indexed Tom), 30, head**, wife read **Malissie, 21**, with terminal letters qualified, and two sons. Both spouses are marked in a first marriage of **three years**. This adds an intermediate household for comparison with the 1900 son, 1918 Thomas Moses/Malissa entries and later adult family; it does not name the head's parents or give the wife's maiden surname.

The original reports **the head born Florida, with both parents born Georgia**, but the sons' rows report their **father born Georgia** and mother Florida. This same-page disagreement remains visible. The older son's fractional age is uncertain and is not normalized here. One household's ages, marriage duration and child count do not establish exact births, a marriage date, biological parentage or the complete later descent. The 1880/1881/1882 birth conflict and shared DNA ancestor remain unresolved.

A separately bounded local reading identified a distinct **Crutchfield household immediately preceding the Pilcher rows**. This is an association lead because the later administrative index names Morris's mother Mary M. Crutchfield. Proximity does not identify this wife or establish kinship; no neighbor family was traced.

**Later certificate-locator successor, 30 September 2026:** The exact [FamilySearch death index entry](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/1:1:VV9B-R8B) now supplies **certificate number 23769** for Thomas Moses Pilcher, 1944, Bay County, plus an indexed **Volume Number 1123** (file FDI03-1944.txt, line 18489). These fields improve the original retrieval target; they were absent from the earlier Ancestry display. FamilySearch explicitly says **No Image Available**, and its collection is the same Ancestry-provided index, not an independent death certificate.

The certificate, named parents and informant remain unread. The indexed volume label is not treated as a physically inspected original register. One official discovery and one exact-name/year record query were completed; no 1944 search was run in the separate Florida certificate collection ending in 1939. No order was sent. The candidate 17 December day still comes from the marker, and the 1880/1881/1882 birth conflict remains unresolved.

### Coverage checklist

The main report covers the American chronology, DNA comparison limits, candidate rankings, tree evidence and fourteen retrieval targets. One hundred dated research notes supply the supporting leads, corrections, bounded negative searches and exact unread references.

**Historical review:** all 181 staged working notes were classified. Of these, 145 map to report sections or research notes; the remaining 36 have explicit historical, background, private-data or operational retention reasons in the restricted topic catalog. This is a topic-level reconciliation, not a claim that every sentence, search attempt or original source has been independently rechecked. Consult the linked working reviews for full detail before repeating a search.

**Now surfaced:** American and overseas birth screens; Aaron’s Carolina and Georgia associates, land, military and estate records; distinct James, David and Devaney identities; Bible provenance and collateral families; comparator pedigrees, geographic clues, surname variants and statistical limits. Search the notes by a surname, place, record collection or note ID. Completed searches retain their date, scope and limits; blocked or unread records remain retrieval targets.

**Still unresolved:** the complete Bible evidence, historical placement of the tested paternal line, comparable terminal DNA evidence, and the original records specified in [Records needed](./index.html#records). Private match identities and detailed DNA data remain in the restricted library. Edition 01.8’s historical search-ledger snapshot contained 11 recorded events across eight search IDs. Subsequent dated notes and restricted supporting reviews preserve newer scopes separately; the historical snapshot is not a complete or current search inventory.
